22	function readSaved(): Saved {
23	  try { return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(KEY) || "{}") as Saved; } catch { return {}; }
24	}
25	function writeSaved(patch: Saved) {
26	  try { localStorage.setItem(KEY, JSON.stringify({ ...readSaved(), ...patch })); } catch { /* storage unavailable */ }
27	}

29	function shuffle<T>(items: T[]): T[] {
30	  const a = [...items];
31	  for (let i = a.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {
32	    const j = Math.floor(Math.random() * (i + 1));
33	    [a[i], a[j]] = [a[j], a[i]];
34	  }
35	  return a;
36	}

import { safeLazy } from '@/lib/safeLazy';

// Bootcamp pages are full-screen SPA routes; they render blank inside an iframe
// (a fresh context loses the session). Mounting the page component inline in the
// same React tree keeps auth/context, so it renders. Shared by the inline-open
// view and the scaled card preview. safeLazy guards against stale post-deploy
// chunks ("Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'default')").

/** Shared notation: slash-through negation, vertical AND (+) and OR stacks. */

export function Tok({ text }: { text: string }) {
  const negated = text.startsWith("/");
  const label = negated ? text.slice(1) : text;
  return negated ? <span className="cir-neg">{label}</span> : <span>{label}</span>;
}

export function Side({ text }: { text: string }) {
  const join = text.includes(" + ") ? "+" : text.includes(" or ") ? "or" : null;
  if (!join) return <Tok text={text.trim()} />;
  const parts = text.split(join === "+" ? " + " : " or ").map(p => p.trim());
  return (
    <span className="cir-stack">
      {parts.map((part, i) => (
        <span key={`${part}-${i}`}>
          {i > 0 && <span className="cir-stack-join">{join}</span>}
          <span style={{ display: "block" }}><Tok text={part} /></span>
        </span>
      ))}
    </span>
  );
}

export function Diagram({ left, right }: { left: string; right: string }) {
  return (
    <span className="cir-diagram">
      <Side text={left} />
      <span className="cir-arrow">&rarr;</span>
      <Side text={right} />
    </span>
  );
}
/* Conditional Indicators Refresher — self-contained paper-and-ink surface.
   Tokens are scoped to the page root so nothing here leaks into the rest of
   the app, and no component hardcodes a colour utility. */

.cir-root {
  --cir-paper: #f6f2e8;
  --cir-paper-raised: #fdfaf2;
  --cir-ink: #13332a;
  --cir-ink-soft: #3c574c;
  --cir-ink-mute: #6d7f77;
  --cir-rule: #d8cfbb;
  --cir-rule-soft: #e7e0d0;
  --cir-good: #1f5c4a;
  --cir-bad: #8d3a33;
  min-height: 100vh;
  background: var(--cir-paper);
  color: var(--cir-ink);
  font-size: 16px;
}

.cir-shell { max-width: 1040px; margin: 0 auto; padding: 32px clamp(16px, 4vw, 44px) 80px; }

.cir-title {
  font-family: var(--font-display, Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif);
  font-size: clamp(30px, 5vw, 46px);
  line-height: 1.1;
  margin: 0 0 4px;
  font-weight: 600;
}
.cir-sub { color: var(--cir-ink-mute); font-size: 14px; margin: 0 0 24px; }

.cir-tabs { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 8px; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--cir-rule); padding-bottom: 12px; margin-bottom: 28px; }
.cir-tab {
  border: 1px solid var(--cir-rule); background: transparent; color: var(--cir-ink-soft);
  border-radius: 999px; padding: 8px 16px; font-size: 14px; cursor: pointer;
}
.cir-tab:hover { border-color: var(--cir-ink-mute); }
.cir-tab[aria-selected="true"] { background: var(--cir-ink); color: var(--cir-paper-raised); border-color: var(--cir-ink); }

.cir-lead { font-family: var(--font-display, Georgia, serif); font-size: clamp(20px, 3vw, 26px); margin: 0 0 20px; }

/* Cheat sheet -------------------------------------------------------------- */
.cir-quadrants { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr)); gap: 16px; }
.cir-quad { background: var(--cir-paper-raised); border: 1px solid var(--cir-rule); border-radius: 14px; padding: clamp(18px, 2.4vw, 26px); }
.cir-quad-n { font-size: 11px; letter-spacing: .14em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--cir-ink-mute); }
.cir-quad h2 { font-family: var(--font-display, Georgia, serif); font-size: 22px; margin: 6px 0 14px; font-weight: 600; }
.cir-words { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 8px; margin-bottom: 16px; }
.cir-word { border: 1px solid var(--cir-rule); border-radius: 8px; padding: 5px 10px; font-size: 14px; background: var(--cir-paper); }
.cir-quad p { color: var(--cir-ink-soft); line-height: 1.65; font-size: 15px; margin: 0; }
.cir-steps { margin: 0; padding-left: 20px; color: var(--cir-ink-soft); line-height: 1.7; font-size: 15px; }
.cir-note { margin-top: 12px; font-size: 14px; color: var(--cir-ink-mute); font-style: italic; }
.cir-legend { margin-top: 22px; border-top: 1px solid var(--cir-rule); padding-top: 18px; display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 28px; align-items: center; color: var(--cir-ink-soft); font-size: 14px; }
.cir-legend > div { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 10px; }

/**
 * Assigning a bootcamp has to actually open it for the student.
 *
 * Three separate places have to agree on one identity: AssignmentBuilder writes
 * the classroom_items row, BootcampGate decides whether to let the student in,
 * and the bootcamp library decides whether to show the card. They agree only if
 * the id derived from the route matches the id stored on the row. These lock
 * that agreement for every registered bootcamp, so a newly added one cannot
 * ship assignable-but-unopenable.
 */

import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import {
  BOOTCAMPS,
  bootcampIdFromRoute,
  classroomItemMatchesBootcamp,
  findBootcampById,
  type BootcampDef,
} from './bootcamps';

/** The classroom_items row AssignmentBuilder creates for a bootcamp. */
function assignedRow(bootcamp: BootcampDef) {
  return {
    source_id: bootcamp.route,
    metadata_json: { bootcamp_id: bootcamp.id } as Record<string, unknown>,
  };
}

describe.each(BOOTCAMPS.map((b) => [b.title, b] as const))('%s is assignable', (_title, bootcamp) => {
  it('derives the same id from its route that assignment stores', () => {
    // Topic-family routes resolve through the registry rather than the path.
    expect(bootcampIdFromRoute(bootcamp.route)).toBe(bootcamp.id);
  });

  it('opens the gate for the row assignment writes', () => {
    expect(classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(assignedRow(bootcamp), bootcamp.id)).toBe(true);
  });

  it('opens the gate when matched by route instead of id', () => {
    expect(classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(assignedRow(bootcamp), bootcamp.route)).toBe(true);
  });

  it('opens the gate on metadata alone', () => {
    expect(
      classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(
        { source_id: null, metadata_json: { bootcamp_id: bootcamp.id } },
        bootcamp.id,
      ),
    ).toBe(true);
  });

  it('opens the gate on source_id alone', () => {
    expect(
      classroomItemMatchesBootcamp({ source_id: bootcamp.route, metadata_json: null }, bootcamp.id),
    ).toBe(true);
  });

  it('does not open any other bootcamp', () => {
    for (const other of BOOTCAMPS) {
      if (other.id === bootcamp.id) continue;
      expect(classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(assignedRow(bootcamp), other.id)).toBe(false);
    }
  });
});

describe('bootcamp registry', () => {
  it('has unique ids and routes', () => {
    expect(new Set(BOOTCAMPS.map((b) => b.id)).size).toBe(BOOTCAMPS.length);
    expect(new Set(BOOTCAMPS.map((b) => b.route)).size).toBe(BOOTCAMPS.length);
  });

  it('registers the RC bootcamp so it appears in the assignment picker', () => {
    const rc = findBootcampById('rc-engaging-with-the-passage');
    expect(rc).toBeDefined();
    expect(rc?.route).toBe('/bootcamp/rc-engaging-with-the-passage');
    expect(rc?.title).toBe('Engaging With The Passage');
  });

  it('shows an assigned bootcamp in the student library', () => {
    // The filter src/pages/Bootcamps.tsx applies for a non-admin.
    const rc = findBootcampById('rc-engaging-with-the-passage')!;
    const items = [{ type: 'bootcamp_module', status: 'assigned', ...assignedRow(rc) }];
    const assignedIds = new Set(
      BOOTCAMPS.filter((bc) =>
        items.some(
          (it) =>
            it.type === 'bootcamp_module' &&
            it.status !== 'archived' &&
            classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(it, bc.id),
        ),
      ).map((bc) => bc.id),
    );
    expect([...assignedIds]).toEqual([rc.id]);
  });
});

  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (authReady && !user) navigate('/auth');
  }, [user, authReady, navigate]);

  const assignedBootcampIds = React.useMemo(() => {
    return new Set(
      BOOTCAMPS.filter((bc) =>

 * The two complete ideas as they appear in the statement: `after` is the idea
 * that follows "unless" (with any slash it carries), `other` is the rest.

export function unlessParts({ pattern, x, y }: UnlessRound): { after: string; other: string } {
  switch (pattern) {

    case 2: return { after: y, other: x };

  }
}

/** The Group 3 move: negate the "unless" idea, put it left, keep the other idea unchanged on the right. */
export function unlessAnswer(round: UnlessRound): { left: string; right: string } {
  const { after, other } = unlessParts(round);
  return { left: negateToken(after), right: other };

export type UnlessVerdict = "correct" | "no-answer" | "not-negated" | "reversed" | "both-negated" | "other";

  const { after, other } = unlessParts(round);

  if (either(after, other)) return "not-negated";
  if (either(other, negateToken(after))) return "reversed";
  if (either(negateToken(after), negateToken(other))) return "both-negated";

}

/* ------------------------------- Unless drill ------------------------------ */

export type UnlessPattern = 1 | 2 | 3 | 4;

export const UNLESS_LETTERS = ["A", "B", "C", "D", "P", "Q", "R", "S"];

export interface UnlessRound {
  pattern: UnlessPattern;
  x: string;
  y: string;
}

/** Statement text pieces; "/" marks the slashed letter. */
export function unlessStatement({ pattern, x, y }: UnlessRound): string {
  switch (pattern) {
    case 1: return `/${x} unless ${y}`;
    case 2: return `Unless ${y}, ${x}`;
    case 3: return `Unless /${y}, /${x}`;
    case 4: return `${x} unless /${y}`;
  }
}

/**
 * The two complete ideas as they appear in the statement: `after` is the idea
 * that follows "unless" (with any slash it carries), `other` is the rest.
 */
export function unlessParts({ pattern, x, y }: UnlessRound): { after: string; other: string } {
  switch (pattern) {
    case 1: return { after: y, other: `/${x}` };
    case 2: return { after: y, other: x };
    case 3: return { after: `/${y}`, other: `/${x}` };
    case 4: return { after: `/${y}`, other: x };
  }
}

/** The Group 3 move: negate the "unless" idea, put it left, keep the other idea unchanged on the right. */
export function unlessAnswer(round: UnlessRound): { left: string; right: string } {
  const { after, other } = unlessParts(round);
  return { left: negateToken(after), right: other };
}

export type UnlessVerdict = "correct" | "no-answer" | "not-negated" | "reversed" | "both-negated" | "other";

/** Grades a placement. The diagram and its contrapositive both count. */
export function gradeUnless(round: UnlessRound, left: string | null, right: string | null): UnlessVerdict {
  if (!left || !right) return "no-answer";
  const { after, other } = unlessParts(round);
  const is = (l: string, r: string) => left === l || right === r ? left === l && right === r : false;
  const either = (l: string, r: string) => is(l, r) || is(negateToken(r), negateToken(l));
  if (either(negateToken(after), other)) return "correct";
  if (either(after, other)) return "not-negated";
  if (either(other, negateToken(after))) return "reversed";
  if (either(negateToken(after), negateToken(other))) return "both-negated";
  return "other";
}

export function negateToken(token: string): string {
  return token.startsWith("/") ? token.slice(1) : `/${token}`;
}

export function contrapositiveOf(left: string, right: string): { left: string; right: string } {
  return { left: negateToken(right), right: negateToken(left) };
}

import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { contrapositiveOf, gradeUnless, unlessAnswer, type UnlessRound } from "./content";

const round = (pattern: 1 | 2 | 3 | 4): UnlessRound => ({ pattern, x: "A", y: "B" });

describe("Unless drill answers follow the Group 3 move", () => {
  // Negate the idea after "unless", put it left, keep the other idea as written.
  it.each([
    [1, "/A unless B", "/B", "/A"],
    [2, "Unless B, A", "/B", "A"],
    [3, "Unless /B, /A", "B", "/A"],
    [4, "A unless /B", "B", "A"],
  ] as const)("pattern %i (%s) is %s -> %s", (pattern, _statement, left, right) => {
    expect(unlessAnswer(round(pattern))).toEqual({ left, right });
  });

  it.each([1, 2, 3, 4] as const)("pattern %i accepts the diagram and its contrapositive", pattern => {
    const r = round(pattern);
    const a = unlessAnswer(r);
    const c = contrapositiveOf(a.left, a.right);
    expect(gradeUnless(r, a.left, a.right)).toBe("correct");
    expect(gradeUnless(r, c.left, c.right)).toBe("correct");
  });

  it("names the common mistakes", () => {
    const r = round(2); // Unless B, A  ->  /B -> A
    expect(gradeUnless(r, null, "A")).toBe("no-answer");
    expect(gradeUnless(r, "B", "A")).toBe("not-negated");
    expect(gradeUnless(r, "A", "/B")).toBe("reversed");
    expect(gradeUnless(r, "/B", "/A")).toBe("both-negated");
    expect(gradeUnless(r, "A", "/A")).toBe("other");
  });
});
EOF

input: {"action":"javascript_exec","tabId":"seed","text":"const sleep = ms => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));\nconst btn = t => [...document.querySelectorAll('button')].find(b => b.textContent.trim().startsWith(t));\nbtn('New round').click();\nawait sleep(2400);\nconst log = [];\nfor (let s = 0; s < 3; s++) {\n  while (!document.querySelector('.cir-statement')) await sleep(30);\n  await sleep(300);\n  const el = document.querySelector('.cir-statement > span');\n  const toks = [...el.childNodes].map(n => (n.querySelector?.('.cir-neg') || n.classList?.contains('cir-neg')) ? '/' + n.textContent : n.textContent);\n  const text = toks.join('');\n  // tokens with letters\n  const letters = toks.filter(t => /^\\/?[A-Z]$/.test(t) && t !== 'U');\n  let after, other;\n  if (text.startsWith('Unless')) { after = letters[0]; other = letters[1]; } else { other = letters[0]; after = letters[1]; }\n  const neg = t => t.startsWith('/') ? t.slice(1) : '/' + t;\n  const want = [neg(after), other];\n  const opts = [...document.querySelectorAll('.ud-token')].map(b => (b.querySelector('.cir-neg') ? '/' : '') + b.firstChild.textContent);\n  for (const w of want) window.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: String(opts.indexOf(w) + 1) }));\n  await sleep(50);\n  window.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: 'Enter' }));\n  log.push(text + ' => ' + want.join(' -> '));\n  await sleep(1000);\n}\nawait sleep(500);\n[log, document.querySelector('.ud-score')?.innerText, localStorage.getItem('lsatu.unless-drill.v1')]"}

## assistant

The Unless drill now has its own page, and you can assign it on its own. It isn't live yet: it's in [PR #205](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/205), which can be merged cleanly and is waiting on you.

**Same questions.** Each round is still three of the four "unless" patterns, with new letters each time and five seconds per statement. I didn't add "until" or "without." The Unless tab inside the refresher now uses the same rebuilt drill.

**Where it lives:** `/unless-drill`. It shows up as **Unless Drill** in Desk → Library → Bootcamps, where you assign it like the refresher. The default end date is 7 days after the start. Students only see it once it's assigned to them.

**The timer and drill, rebuilt:**
- **Clock:** a 3-2-1 "get ready" count, then a countdown with a bar that drains and turns red in the last 1.5 seconds. "Lock in" ends a statement early, so the time recorded is how fast they actually were. There's also an optional 10-second warm-up pace.
- **Answering:** students tap an idea to fill the left side, then the right. Tapping a side clears it. Keys 1–4, Backspace and Enter work too.
- **Before starting:** the four steps of the Group 3 move, with a worked example.
- **Review:** score, average time and fastest perfect round. Each statement shows the correct diagram and the contrapositive (both count as correct). It also names the mistake: didn't negate, wrong direction, negated both, or ran out of time. Then it works the move through on that statement. "Retry the ones I missed" reruns only the missed statements.

**What was broken before:**
- A style rule shrank the statement and diagrams in the review to tiny grey text. That's the garbled "Received" and "Correct" in your screenshot.
- The drill could record each answer twice.
- The step lists had lost their numbers.

**Checks:** the code compiles with no type or lint errors, and 66 tests pass, including 9 new ones for the answer key and grading. I ran the drill in a browser and checked a timeout, a wrong-direction answer, an all-correct round and phone width.

**Not checked:** I couldn't open the real signed-in `/unless-drill` page. I tested the drill by mounting the page on its own in a local test file, which I've deleted.

To get it to students I need two things from you:
1. **OK to merge PR #205 and publish.** Publishing the site doesn't spend Lovable credits.
2. **Which students to assign it to.**

The revised local copy asks what the learner needs to decide: “Who does the rule say will receive a wristband?”, “What kind of key is needed to open the cabinet?”, and “What kind of pass does entry require?” The lesson demonstrates why before noon, current, registered and with yellow tags matter. Dropping one of those words changes which cases the rule covers. An arrow is checked by imagining its left condition and asking what the sentence then establishes, rather than treating the first English clause as the left side.

Joshua also asked for the mixed drill to train a skill and build a habit, with one question visible and a progress bar. The current local response uses five stages with a concrete purpose for each: retain the full conditions, build a diagram, attempt a translation before supplied labels, compare meanings, then transfer to unfamiliar cases. Error feedback distinguishes dropped qualifications, reversed roles, required versus enough and changed meaning. A fresh question checks the repaired distinction; correcting the exposed answer alone does not demonstrate transfer. Written translations are compared by the learner and are not represented as automatically graded.

Keep modality in the examples. An electric vehicle that is denied permission disproves the claim that being electric guarantees permission; a vehicle merely staying outside does not. A required action is an obligation, not a fact proving compliance. Confirmation practice for that mistake must test another obligation rather than an unrelated wording comparison.

These are demonstrated corrections and the current implementation response, not approval of every exact replacement sentence or proof that the voice matches Joshua identically. The [selected voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) still governs wording, cadence, concrete reasoning and scope. [Current session record](log.md) distinguishes local work from publication.

Further steering in the same session: “Keep the complete condition” was itself awkward as a visible heading. Ask who or what the particular rule covers instead. Joshua also rejected the landing page's total question/stage counts, per-stage counts, long stage list and padded explanation of repairs. A shorter launch should explain the immediate task; each stage should introduce its purpose and instructions when the learner reaches it. That latest flow revision was requested while final verification was still pending, so this record does not certify its implementation.


### RC actions should describe what the student gets (2026-10-07)

Keep Start this drill distinct and explain whether answers are hidden and whether work is saved. Generic Menu is also insufficient where the actual action is Question details. This correction does not justify adding more launch instructions, repeated summaries, or unrelated assigned work inside RC setup.

command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-unless && cat >> src/features/conditional-refresher/UnlessDrill.tsx <<'EOF'

/* A looping preview of one statement, played on the real drill furniture. */
const PREVIEW = { pattern: 1 as const, x: "K", y: "M" }; // /K, unless M.  ->  K -> M
const PREVIEW_LOOP_MS = 7000;
const PREVIEW_BEATS = [
  { at: 0, caption: "The statement appears and the clock starts." },
  { at: 1400, caption: "Choose an idea and negate it: /K becomes K. Tap it for the left." },
  { at: 2900, caption: "Tap the other idea, M, for the right. It stays exactly as written." },
  { at: 4300, caption: "Lock it in." },
  { at: 4900, caption: "Correct. Your review shows every statement after the round." },
];

function DrillPreview() {
  const reduced = useMemo(
    () => typeof window !== "undefined" && window.matchMedia?.("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)").matches,
    [],
  );
  const [t, setT] = useState(reduced ? 5000 : 0);
  useEffect(() => {
    if (reduced) return;
    const start = performance.now();
    const id = window.setInterval(() => setT((performance.now() - start) % PREVIEW_LOOP_MS), 80);
    return () => window.clearInterval(id);
  }, [reduced]);

  const beat = PREVIEW_BEATS.reduce((b, x, i) => (t >= x.at ? i : b), 0);
  const left = beat >= 1 ? "K" : null;
  const right = beat >= 2 ? "M" : null;
  const pressed = beat === 1 ? "K" : beat === 2 ? "M" : null;
  const locked = beat >= 3;
  const remaining = Math.max(0, 5000 - Math.min(t, PREVIEW_BEATS[3].at));
  const fraction = remaining / 5000;

  return (
    <div className="ud-preview" aria-label="Preview of one statement in the drill">
      <div className="ud-preview-screen" aria-hidden="true">
        <div className="ud-head">
          <span className="cir-meta">Statement 1 of 4</span>
          <span className="ud-count">{locked ? "Locked" : (remaining / 1000).toFixed(1)}</span>
        </div>
        <div className="ud-clock"><span style={{ transform: `scaleX(${fraction})` }} /></div>
        <p className="cir-statement"><UnlessText round={PREVIEW} /></p>
        <div className="cir-build ud-build">
          <span className="cir-slot ud-slot">{left ? <Tok text={left} /> : <span className="cir-stack-join">left</span>}</span>
          <span className="cir-arrow">&rarr;</span>
          <span className="cir-slot ud-slot">{right ? <Tok text={right} /> : <span className="cir-stack-join">right</span>}</span>
        </div>
        <div className="ud-tokens">
          {["K", "M", "/K", "/M"].map(o => (
            <span key={o} className={`cir-token ud-token${pressed === o ? " is-pressed" : ""}`}><Tok text={o} /></span>
          ))}
        </div>
        <div className="cir-actions ud-lock">
          <span className={`cir-btn${beat === 3 ? " is-pressed" : ""}`}>Lock in</span>
        </div>
        {beat >= 4 && <p className="ud-preview-result">Correct: <Diagram left="K" right="M" /></p>}
      </div>
      <p className="ud-preview-caption" aria-live="off">{PREVIEW_BEATS[beat].caption}</p>
    </div>
  );
}
EOF
cat >> src/features/conditional-refresher/conditional-refresher.css <<'EOF'

/* Unless drill preview clip ------------------------------------------------ */
.ud-preview { margin: 0 0 18px; }
.ud-preview-screen { position: relative; text-align: center; background: var(--cir-paper-raised); border: 1px solid var(--cir-rule); border-radius: 12px; padding: 16px 18px 18px; pointer-events: none; user-select: none; }
.ud-preview-screen .cir-statement { font-size: clamp(22px, 4vw, 30px); margin: 12px 0 16px; }
.ud-preview-screen .ud-token { font-size: 20px; padding: 10px 0; min-height: 0; transition: transform .15s ease, border-color .15s ease; }
.ud-preview-screen .is-pressed { transform: scale(.94); border-color: var(--cir-ink); }
.ud-preview-screen .cir-btn { display: inline-block; min-width: 140px; transition: transform .15s ease; }
.ud-preview-result { position: absolute; left: 50%; bottom: 14px; transform: translateX(-50%); margin: 0; display: inline-flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px; background: var(--cir-paper-raised); border: 1px solid var(--cir-good); color: var(--cir-good); border-radius: 999px; padding: 6px 16px; font-weight: 600; white-space: nowrap; }
.ud-preview-result .cir-diagram { font-size: 18px; }
.ud-preview-caption { margin: 10px 0 0; font-size: 14px; color: var(--cir-ink-soft); text-align: center; min-height: 2.6em; }
EOF

## assistant

I took out the written example and put a short looping clip in its place. It plays one statement on the real drill screen, so students see exactly what they're about to do. It's pushed to [PR #205](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/205).

**What the clip shows** (about 7 seconds, on repeat, starting as soon as the page opens):
1. "/K, unless M." appears and the clock starts draining.
2. K gets tapped and drops into the left side.
3. M gets tapped and drops into the right side.
4. Lock in gets pressed.
5. "Correct: K → M" appears.

A one-line caption under the clip says what's happening at each step. It uses its own letters (K and M), not A and B.

**The start screen now has:** the title, the four steps, the clip, the tap/lock-in line, the pace choice, your "start every study session" line, and Start round.

**Checked:** type check and lint pass. I watched it in the browser and every step played in order, ending on "Correct: K → M." For students whose device is set to reduce motion, it shows the finished step instead of animating. I didn't check that setting in the browser.

It's still not live for students. Say merge and publish, and tell me which students should get it.

## user

There should be a play button for this. There should be a play button, like for plays. And just make it clear this is a preview, because it looks like it's just—it looks like it's running. So it should be a little "preview" label. And the first time they see the drill, they should first have to clear it doing 10 seconds and get it perfectly, then they unlock 5 seconds. Have that set up too.

-- Topic-bootcamp progress tracking. Two thin tables:
--
-- 1. chapter_progress
--    One row per (user, topic_tag, section_id) the first time a student
--    scrolls a chapter section into view. Updated on subsequent views.
--    Powers the Teacher Workspace Bootcamps dashboard "sections viewed".
--
-- 2. flashcard_reviews
--    One row per flashcard review event. SRS-compatible: stores the rating
--    multiplier the student picked, the next-due timestamp, and the
--    cumulative review_count. Aggregations (mastery %, due count, avg
--    interval) compose at read time so future SRS changes do not break
--    historical data.
--
-- Both tables: RLS so a student can read+write only their own rows; admin
-- can read everyone via public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin').

-- ── chapter_progress ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.chapter_progress (
  user_id          uuid        NOT NULL REFERENCES auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  topic_tag        text        NOT NULL,
  section_id       text        NOT NULL,
  first_viewed_at  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  last_viewed_at   timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  view_count       integer     NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
  PRIMARY KEY (user_id, topic_tag, section_id)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS chapter_progress_topic_user_idx
  ON public.chapter_progress (topic_tag, user_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS chapter_progress_topic_last_viewed_idx
  ON public.chapter_progress (topic_tag, last_viewed_at DESC);

ALTER TABLE public.chapter_progress ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

DROP POLICY IF EXISTS "chapter_progress_select_own" ON public.chapter_progress;
CREATE POLICY "chapter_progress_select_own"
  ON public.chapter_progress FOR SELECT
  TO authenticated
  USING (auth.uid() = user_id);

DROP POLICY IF EXISTS "chapter_progress_select_admin" ON public.chapter_progress;
CREATE POLICY "chapter_progress_select_admin"
  ON public.chapter_progress FOR SELECT
  TO authenticated
  USING (public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin'));

DROP POLICY IF EXISTS "chapter_progress_insert_own" ON public.chapter_progress;
CREATE POLICY "chapter_progress_insert_own"
  ON public.chapter_progress FOR INSERT
  TO authenticated
  WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id);

DROP POLICY IF EXISTS "chapter_progress_update_own" ON public.chapter_progress;
CREATE POLICY "chapter_progress_update_own"
  ON public.chapter_progress FOR UPDATE
  TO authenticated
  USING (auth.uid() = user_id)
  WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id);

> **Additional context needed**: quality bar (MVP vs flagship).

Perform a meticulous final pass to catch all the small details that separate good work from great work. The difference between shipped and polished.

## Design System Discovery

Aligning the feature to the design system is **not optional**. Polish without alignment is decoration on top of drift, and it makes the next person's job harder. Discovery comes before any other polish work.

1. **Find the design system**: Search for design system documentation, component libraries, style guides, or token definitions. Study the core patterns: design principles, target audience, color tokens, spacing scale, typography styles, component API, motion conventions.
2. **Note the conventions**: How are shared components imported? What spacing scale is used? Which colors come from tokens vs hard-coded values? What motion and interaction patterns are established? What flow shapes are used for comparable actions (modal vs full-page, inline vs route, save-on-blur vs explicit submit)?
3. **Identify drift, then name the root cause**: For every deviation, classify it as a **missing token** (the value should exist in the system but doesn't), a **one-off implementation** (a shared component already exists but wasn't used), or a **conceptual misalignment** (the feature's flow, IA, or hierarchy doesn't match neighboring features). The fix differs by category — patch the value, swap to the shared component, or rework the flow. Fixing the symptom without naming the cause is how drift compounds.

If a design system exists, polish **must** align the feature with it. If none exists, polish against the conventions visible in the codebase. **If anything about the system is ambiguous, ask — never guess at design system principles.**

## Pre-Polish Assessment

Understand the current state and goals before touching anything:

1. **Review completeness**:
   - Is it functionally complete?
   - Are there known issues to preserve (mark with TODOs)?
   - What's the quality bar? (MVP vs flagship feature?)
   - When does it ship? (How much time for polish?)

2. **Think experience-first**: Who actually uses this, and what's the best possible experience for them? Effective design beats decorative polish — a feature that looks beautiful but fights the user's flow is not polished. Walk the path from their perspective before opening DevTools.

3. **Identify polish areas**:
   - Visual inconsistencies
   - Spacing and alignment issues
   - Interaction state gaps
   - Copy inconsistencies
   - Edge cases and error states
   - Loading and transition smoothness
   - Information architecture and flow drift (does this feature reveal complexity the way neighboring features do?)

4. **Triage cosmetic vs functional**: Classify each issue as **cosmetic** (looks off, doesn't impede the user) or **functional** (breaks, blocks, or confuses the experience). When polish time is tight, functional issues ship first; cosmetic ones can land in a follow-up. Quality should be consistent — never perfect one corner while leaving another rough.

**CRITICAL**: Polish is the last step, not the first. Don't polish work that's not functionally complete.

## Polish Systematically

Work through these dimensions methodically:

### Visual Alignment & Spacing

- **Pixel-perfect alignment**: Everything lines up to grid
- **Consistent spacing**: All gaps use spacing scale (no random 13px gaps)
- **Optical alignment**: Adjust for visual weight (icons may need offset for optical centering)
- **Responsive consistency**: Spacing and alignment work at all breakpoints
- **Grid adherence**: Elements snap to baseline grid

**Check**:
- Enable grid overlay and verify alignment
- Check spacing with browser inspector
- Test at multiple viewport sizes
- Look for elements that "feel" off

### Information Architecture & Flow

### State (semantic only, never decorative)

- **Correct** (`#10b77f`) and **Incorrect** (`#f43e5c`): a student's attempt was right or wrong. Held under their own names, separate from system status, so a future shift to one cannot silently reframe the other.
- **Warn** (`#f59f0a`): a bonus, a stretch, a non-blocking notice.
- **Success** (`#21c45d`), **Warning** (`#f59f0a`), **Destructive** (`#dc2828`): system status, not student performance.

### Named Rules

**The Ground Decides Rule.** A warm colour is legible on the Hall's night and illegible on the Workroom's page. Never carry a colour across the seam on the strength of liking it; measure it against the ground it will land on first.

**The Meaning-Only Rule.** A colour appears in the Workroom because something is right, wrong, cautionary, or dangerous. There is no fifth reason. If a surface feels flat and the impulse is to add a colour, the answer is type and space.

**The Named Ground Rule.** No colour is written as a literal hex in a component. It resolves from a token, and a token that resolves to nothing is a defect, not a subtle effect. Three colour names in this codebase pointed at variables that were never defined, and the product silently lost its focus ring for five months.

## Typography
## Elevation & Depth

**Flat, with hairline separation.** Surfaces sit on the page rather than above it. A card is distinguished from its ground by a one-pixel border and, where more is needed, by one step of background tone. There are no drop shadows in the Workroom.

This is a decision, not a description. The codebase currently ships five shadow tokens tuned for a dark ground, from `0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` up to `0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.7)`, and applies the smallest of them to every card. Because the app ships light by default, those shadows land at 40 percent black under a white card. The light theme never redefined them, and the one glow token is white, so it is invisible on white. The tokens are retired by this record and the code has not caught up yet.

The Hall is the exception and keeps its own depth: fixed ambience gradients, a grain overlay, and an inset highlight on its call to action. That depth is atmospheric rather than structural, and it does not transfer.

### Named Rules

**The Flat Rule.** Depth in the Workroom comes from a hairline or a tonal step. A `box-shadow` on a Workroom surface is a defect.

**The Two-Step Rule.** Page, then card, then at most one raised step. A surface that needs a fourth level of layering is a surface that needs restructuring.

## Shapes

Corners are gently rounded and consistent: 8px for cards and containers, 6px for buttons and inputs, 4px for the smallest chips and insets, and a full pill only for genuine badges. The scale derives from one 0.5rem radius token, so a change to the base moves the whole system together.

The Hall uses a near-square 2px radius instead. Its edges are cut, not softened, which is what separates a carved surface from a card.

Borders are one pixel. There is no second border weight in the Workroom.

### Named Rules

**The Hairline Rule.** One pixel, always. A thicker border used as a coloured accent stripe on the leading edge of a surface is banned, including when faked with an inset shadow. Active state is carried by background tone.

## Components

### Buttons

- **Character:** precise and quiet. A button is a plain rectangle with a label. It darkens slightly on hover and does nothing else. It does not lift, does not cast a shadow, and does not scale on press.
- **Shape:** gently rounded (6px).
- **Primary:** ink fill (`#121212`), page-coloured label, 8px by 16px padding.
- **Outline:** page background, one-pixel field border, ink label.
- **Ghost:** no fill at rest, a tonal fill on hover.
- **Sizes:** default 44px tall, small 40px, large 48px, icon 44 by 44. Every size clears the 44px touch minimum by being that height, never by relying on wrapped text or padding accidents.
- **Focus:** a solid two-...

## assistant

The 10-then-5 unlock is now saved to each student's account, and I did the polish pass with the impeccable skill. Both are pushed to [PR #205](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/205).

**How the unlock works now:**
1. Every student starts at 10 seconds. 5 seconds is locked.
2. One full round of all four, 100% correct at 10 seconds, unlocks 5 seconds. The review says "5 seconds is unlocked" and offers "Try 5 seconds." Retrying only the missed statements doesn't count.
3. All four correct at 5 seconds clears the drill. The start screen then says "Cleared at 5 seconds. Keep it as your warm-up."

Both milestones are saved to the student's account, so they carry across devices. I used a progress table that already exists, so no new database setup is needed. Each student can only see their own record, and you can see everyone's.

**Not checked:** I couldn't test saving to a real signed-in account, because that would mean signing in as a student. I tested it signed out, where it falls back to saving in the browser. The save code follows the pattern the bootcamps already use, but the first real proof will be a student unlocking 5 seconds after it's live.

**Polish fixes**, checked against your app's design rules:
- **Review rows:** the colored stripe on the left edge is a pattern your design file bans. Each row now has a small "Correct" or "Not correct" label instead.
- **Correct rows are shorter:** they show the student's diagram and its contrapositive. The repeated answer and the worked steps now only appear on misses.
- **Score:** a plain line of numbers instead of three boxes.
- **Steps list:** no longer a box inside a box.
- **Flat surfaces:** removed the shadow on the play button and the shrink-on-press effect, and the left/right slots now use thin 1px lines.
- **Buttons:** every button is now at least 44px tall, easy to tap on a phone.
- **Keyboard and motion:** every control shows a visible outline when reached by keyboard, and animations turn off for students who've asked their device to reduce motion.
- **Preview clip:** its clock now starts at 10.0 to match where students begin, and its left and right slots stay on one line on a phone.

**Checked:** type check, lint and all 66 tests pass. In the browser I went through a fresh start, a mixed round, a perfect 10-second round that unlocked 5 (including a contrapositive answer graded as correct), the review screen, and phone width with no sideways scrolling.

To get it to students, I still need your OK to merge and publish, and the names of the students who should get it.

  return (
    <section className="flex h-full w-full flex-col">
      <header className="shrink-0 border-b border-black/10 px-6 py-4">
        <h2 className="text-base font-semibold text-black">Bootcamps</h2>
        <p className="mt-0.5 text-xs text-black/55">
          Open a bootcamp to preview it, or assign it to one or more students with a start and end date.
        </p>
      </header>

      <div className="min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto px-6 py-5">
        <LibraryViews
          view={view}
          items={BOOTCAMPS.map((b): LibraryItem => ({
            id: b.id,
            title: b.title,
            subtitle: b.description,
            renderPreview: ({ live }) => <BootcampCardPreview bootcamp={b} live={live} />,
            actions: (
              <>
                <button
                  type="button"
                  onClick={() => setOpenBootcamp(b)}
                  className="inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 border px-2.5 py-1 text-xs font-semibold shadow-sm transition hover:brightness-105"
                  style={openButtonStyle(b)}
                >
                  <Play className="h-3 w-3" /> Open
                </button>
                <button
                  type="button"
                  onClick={() => openAssign(b)}
                  className="ml-auto inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 border border-teacher-accent/40 bg-teacher-accent/15 px-2.5 py-1 text-xs font-semibold text-teacher-accent-strong transition hover:bg-teacher-accent/25"
                >
                  <Send className="h-3 w-3" /> Assign
                </button>
              </>
            ),
          }))}
        />
      </div>

      {/* Assign sheet */}
      <Sheet open={!!assignBootcamp} onOpenChange={(o) => !o && closeAssign()}>
  return (
    // No header of its own: the classroom rail owns the way back, sign out and
    // the theme toggle. Drawing them again put a second set on top of the frame.
    <div className="bg-background">
      <section aria-labelledby="bootcamps-heading" className="max-w-7xl mx-auto space-y-6">
        <div>
          <h2 id="bootcamps-heading" className="text-3xl font-semibold tracking-tight text-foreground">Bootcamps</h2>
          <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground mt-1.5">
            {is_admin
              ? 'All bootcamps. Students only see bootcamps you assign from the Teacher Workspace.'
              : 'Focused skill-building programs assigned to you by Joshua.'}

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
<system-reminder>This memory is 16 days old. Memories are point-in-time observations, not live state — claims about code behavior or file:line citations may be outdated. Verify against current code before asserting as fact.</system-reminder>
1	---
2	name: lovable-frontend-publish-is-separate
3	description: A push builds a Lovable preview but never reaches lsatprep.study; publish with deploy_project, wait for the sync, and prove it from the bundle.
4	metadata:
5	  type: project
6	---
7	
8	Pushing to `main` makes Lovable build the commit — its project record shows a
9	preview screenshot tagged with the commit sha within minutes — but the published
10	site at lsatprep.study keeps serving the previous bundle until something calls
11	`deploy_project` (Lovable MCP, `project_id 53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`,
12	name `lsatu`). Confirmed 2026-09-08: a fix sat built-but-unpublished for 20
13	minutes with no error anywhere.
14	
15	**The sync lags the push.** `deploy_project` publishes whatever Lovable has
16	ingested, not what is on origin. On 2026-09-09 a deploy fired seconds after a
17	successful push and shipped the *previous* commit; `get_project` showed
18	`latest_commit_sha` catching up about three minutes later, and a second deploy
19	was needed. There is no error and no warning — the deploy reports `pending` and
20	succeeds, just on the wrong commit.
21	
22	**Why:** build, sync and publish are three separate steps, and none of them
23	reports that the others have not happened. A green push reads as shipped.
24	
25	**How to apply:** before calling `deploy_project`, call `get_project` and check
26	`latest_commit_sha` equals your HEAD; if it does not, wait and re-check. After
27	deploying, prove it from the bundle: pick a string **the commit alone**
28	introduces — not a class name an earlier deploy already shipped, which is how a
29	stale bundle passed a check on 2026-09-09 — then fetch `https://lsatprep.study/`,
30	read the `index-*` asset hash out of it, and grep the asset for the marker.
31	Allow several minutes; the hash changing is the signal a publish landed at all.
32	Note `deploy_project` ships every unpublished commit on main, not just yours.
33	
34	Same trap, different half: [[lovable-edge-functions-do-not-deploy-from-push]].
35	Related: [[push-only-your-own-commits]].
36	
37	**Standing permission (Joshua, 2026-09-26):** publishing the frontend with the
38	free `deploy_project` is pre-authorized, so he does not need to press Publish
39	himself. It applies only to that free path. Anything that spends or might spend
40	Lovable credits still needs his confirmation right before it runs
41	([[minimize-lovable-credits]]). If he opens Lovable after one of these publishes,
42	it will show nothing to publish, and that is expected.
43	
[/external_agent_tool_result]

- **Merged to main:** [PR #205](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/205), the drill.

- **Quick Drills section:** both your Desk library and the student library now have a Quick Drills section next to Bootcamps, and Unless Drill sits there. For students, its button says "Start drill" instead of "Launch Bootcamp."
- **Assigned to Zeina** (zeina.borji5@gmail.com). Her inbox message, bell notification and email all went through, and I confirmed it's in her Classroom. Her account already had the access the drill needs. I didn't set a due date because you didn't give one. Tell me a date and I'll add it.

**Things I had to fix or work around to get here:**
- **The assign command couldn't assign drills like this one.** It only handled activities, guided drills, sets and messages. I added a bootcamp option in a second change, [PR #206](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/206), which is also merged. It assigns through the same app code as the Assign button in your Desk.
- **I published too early the first time.** I published seconds after merging, before Lovable had picked up the new code, which is the exact mistake my notes warn about. That first publish shipped the old version. I waited until Lovable had the right version, published again, and confirmed the drill is now on the live site.
- **The usual assign tool refused to run.** Its working copy has unsaved edits to three hook settings files that I didn't make, and the same edits appear in your main LSAT U folder. I didn't throw those edits away. I ran the assignment from my own clean, up-to-date copy of main instead, but the usual tool will keep refusing until those edits are kept or discarded. Tell me which and I'll do it.
- **A full test run turned up 20 failing tests** in the activity-centre code. That code doesn't use anything I changed. All the drill and library tests pass.
